Becker Muscular Dystrophy

Mendelian MONDO:0010311 Pathograph 24 Show in embeddings browser Dystrophinopathy Muscular Dystrophy

Becker muscular dystrophy is an X-linked dystrophinopathy caused by pathogenic DMD variants that permit production of partly functional dystrophin. Protein quantity, structure, and clinical severity vary. In-frame variants predominate, but the reading-frame rule is predictive rather than diagnostic: out-of-frame, nonsense, splice, and other variants can also produce a Becker phenotype. Progressive proximal weakness, calf hypertrophy, exercise-related symptoms, or incidental hyperCKaemia may lead to diagnosis. Cardiomyopathy can be severe despite relatively mild skeletal muscle involvement. Age at loss of ambulation and respiratory impairment vary substantially between patients and genotypes.

Inheritance

1
X-linked recessive HP:0001419
Inheritance is X-linked. A heterozygous mother has a 50% chance of transmitting the variant in each pregnancy; an affected father transmits it to all daughters and no sons. Heterozygous females may have cardiac disease, muscle symptoms, or rarely a more severe dystrophinopathy phenotype. A negative family history does not exclude the disorder.
X-linked recessive inheritance
Show evidence (2 references)
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK1119/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK1119.pdf SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"If the mother of the proband has a DMD pathogenic variant, the chance of the mother transmitting it in each pregnancy is 50%. If the father of the proband has a DMD pathogenic variant, he will transmit it to all his daughters and none of his sons."
GeneReviews states parental transmission probabilities explicitly.
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK1119/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK1119.pdf SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Heterozygous females are typically asymptomatic or may exhibit weakness, fatigue, and cardiac involvement."
The female phenotype extends beyond isolated cardiac risk and is variably expressed.

Discussions and Knowledge Gaps

3
Should Becker muscular dystrophy be curated as the therapeutic target state of exon-skipping antisense therapy in Duchenne muscular dystrophy, and does that make it a conformer of the antisense-oligonucleotide module?
INTERPRETATION OPEN interpretation_becker_is_the_target_state_of_dmd_exon_skipping
Attached to
pathophysiology#Reduced or Abnormal Partly Functional Dystrophin
Some Duchenne exon-skipping therapies restore an open reading frame and aim to produce partly functional, Becker-like dystrophin. Becker natural history and genotype-specific models can inform expectations, but an induced protein is not guaranteed to reproduce a particular Becker clinical trajectory. This untreated disease entry does not assert an antisense intervention in its causal path.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:16770791 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"In Becker patients, in-frame mutations allow the synthesis of internally deleted, but largely functional dystrophins."
The protein state that exon skipping in Duchenne is designed to produce, stated here as the natural state in Becker.
PMID:20301298 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Dystrophin restoration therapies have been developed by using synthetic antisense oligonucleotides to restore the reading frame by exon skipping for individuals with specific pathogenic variants in DMD."
The source describes the reading-frame-restoration strategy in Duchenne; it does not establish treatment of Becker patients or guarantee a specific clinical outcome.
How should exceptions to the reading-frame rule be curated, given that a substantial minority of clinically definite Becker patients carry out-of-frame mutations?
INTERPRETATION OPEN interpretation_reading_frame_rule_is_statistical
Attached to
pathophysiology#DMD Pathogenic Variant
Reading-frame concordance is a statistical association: the 1989 deletion series reported 92%, and the Leiden database reported 91%. The Italian Becker cohort separately contained out-of-frame and nonsense variants among clinically characterized patients. These percentages have different denominators and cannot be pooled. Alternative splicing, translational re-initiation, and distal truncation can help explain exceptions, but the rescue mechanism must be demonstrated for the specific variant. Phenotype, dystrophin protein, and molecular findings should be interpreted together. Mild or asymptomatic in-frame deletion carriers may also be underascertained.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:2491009 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The correlation between phenotype and type of deletion mutation is in agreement with the "reading frame" theory in 92% of cases and is of diagnostic and prognostic significance."
The deletion series supports a useful but incomplete association between reading frame and phenotype.
PMID:16770791 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"over 4700 mutations have been reported in the Leiden DMD mutation database, of which 91% are in agreement with this rule."
The larger mutation database reports a similar but distinct concordance estimate with a different denominator.
PMID:2491009 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The distribution and frequency of deletions spanning the entire locus suggests that many "in-frame" deletions of the dystrophin gene are not detected because the individuals bearing them are either asymptomatic or exhibit non-DMD/non-BMD clinical features."
The ascertainment argument, made by the authors themselves, which bears on how the 92% should be read.
Does sustained correction of exercise vascular dysregulation improve strength or slow progression in defined Becker genotypes?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN gap_vascular_rescue_and_long_term_becker_outcomes
Attached to
pathophysiology#Impaired Exercise-Induced Vasomodulation
Acute tadalafil and nitrate studies support an NO-dependent vascular mechanism, but they do not establish clinically meaningful disease modification. A four-week sildenafil trial was negative. Differences in intervention, duration, target abundance, and endpoints limit direct comparisons.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:23197572 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The present study does not address whether restoring sympatholysis with PDE5A inhibition can slow disease progression and improve muscle strength."
The investigators explicitly distinguish vascular rescue from clinical efficacy.
PMID:25042931 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Sixteen patients completed all skeletal muscle evaluations, and 13 completed the cardiac MRI investigations. Sildenafil had no effect on any of the outcome parameters."
The four-week crossover study found no improvement in the measured muscular or cardiac outcomes.
⚙

Pathophysiology

10
DMD Pathogenic Variant
Pathogenic variants in DMD alter dystrophin quantity or function. In-frame deletions and duplications are common, but neither reading frame nor variant position alone establishes the Becker phenotype. The Italian cohort included 779 in-frame, 89 out-of-frame, and 35 nonsense variants among 903 genetically characterized patients. Particular exon deletions correlate with clinical outcomes at the group level; substantial variation remains within genotypes and families.
Downstream
Reduced or Abnormal Partly Functional Dystrophin DMD variants can produce reduced amounts of dystrophin, an abnormal protein, or both, while retaining sufficient function for a Becker clinical phenotype. The protein outcome cannot be inferred solely from reading-frame preservation.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:3384440 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Deletions identified in three BMD patients are shown to maintain the translational ORF for amino acids and predict a shorter, lower molecular weight protein."
The original statement of the reading-frame model, on the Becker side of the comparison.
PMID:39499670 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Ten per cent of patients carried out-of-frame mutations, 4% nonsense mutations and 86% in-frame deletions/duplications."
The mutational composition of a 943-patient Becker cohort. It gives both the dominant in-frame class and the size of the exception to the rule.
PMID:39499670 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Patients carrying del45-49 compared with del45-47 were associated with an earlier loss of ambulation (P = 1 × 10-4), whereas patients with del45-55 (P = 0.005), del48 (P = 0.02) and del48-49 (P = 0.02) were correlated with a later loss of ambulation compared with del45-47."
Within-class genotype-phenotype resolution: which exons are removed changes the trajectory even when the frame is preserved throughout.
Reduced or Abnormal Partly Functional Dystrophin
Residual dystrophin distinguishes the Becker phenotype from severe dystrophin deficiency, but its abundance and function vary. Internally deleted dystrophin is a common outcome, not the only one. In bmx mice with deletion of Dmd exons 45-47, skeletal and cardiac dystrophin protein is reduced despite preserved transcript abundance. Sarcolemmal nNOS localization is lost and alpha-sarcoglycan localization is reduced in this model; these allele-specific observations should not be assumed for every Becker variant.
dystrophin-glycoprotein complex at the sarcolemma GO:0016010 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cellular component This pathophysiological event involves dystrophin-glycoprotein complex at the sarcolemma, annotated with dystrophin-associated glycoprotein complex (GO:0016010). GO:0016010 is a cellular component from the Gene Ontology.
Animal models
bmx exon 45-47 deletion mouse Mouse partially recapitulates Reproduces the protein deficit with preserved transcript abundance.
Downstream
Contraction-Induced Skeletal Muscle Injury Partly functional dystrophin provides incomplete protection against contraction-induced skeletal muscle injury. The exon 45-47 bmx model retains susceptibility to eccentric contractions.
Dystrophin-Deficient Cardiomyocyte Injury The dystrophin deficit also affects cardiomyocytes and can cause cardiac dysfunction despite relatively preserved skeletal muscle function.
Loss of Sarcolemmal nNOS Localization Some DMD variants disrupt dystrophin-dependent membrane localization of nNOS. Human biopsy evidence shows this for several exon-deletion groups, while other Becker variants retain sarcolemmal nNOS.
Show evidence (5 references)
PMID:2491009 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"For cases with a deletion mutation, the "reading frame" hypothesis predicts that BMD patients produce a semifunctional, internally deleted dystrophin protein, whereas DMD patients produce a severely truncated protein that would be unstable."
The contrast at protein level between the two diseases, which is what this node models.
PMID:16770791 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"In Becker patients, in-frame mutations allow the synthesis of internally deleted, but largely functional dystrophins."
Restatement of the same protein-level claim by a group curating the mutation database.
PMID:39499670 SUPPORT BACKGROUND Human Clinical
"Becker muscular dystrophy (BMD) is an X-linked neuromuscular disease attributable to mutations in DMD, leading to a deficient and less functional dystrophin, mainly in skeletal and cardiac muscle."
States both the protein state and the two tissues it matters in, which is where this node branches.
+ 2 more references
Contraction-Induced Skeletal Muscle Injury
Dystrophin supports muscle membrane stability. In Becker muscular dystrophy, residual protein does not fully prevent contraction-related injury. Exon 45-47 bmx mouse muscle shows increased force loss after eccentric contractions, raised serum creatine kinase, necrotic and inflammatory foci, regeneration, and fibrosis. The severity of individual measurements need not lie uniformly between wild type and dystrophin-null muscle.
skeletal muscle fibre CL:0008002 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves skeletal muscle fibre, annotated with skeletal muscle fiber (CL:0008002). CL:0008002 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
Animal models
bmx exon 45-47 deletion mouse Mouse partially recapitulates Eccentric contraction experiments show greater force loss than wild type.
Downstream
Slowly Progressive Proximal Myopathy Recurrent injury, degeneration, and fibrofatty replacement contribute to progressive loss of skeletal muscle function; the pace varies across patients.
Elevated Creatine Kinase Circulating creatine kinase is a marker of skeletal muscle injury in Becker muscular dystrophy. Its level does not directly measure remaining strength and can decline as muscle is replaced by fat.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:16770791 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"This gene codes for dystrophin, a protein important for maintaining the stability of muscle-fiber membranes."
Dystrophin's function at the muscle-fibre membrane, which is what a semifunctional protein partly preserves.
PMID:36628607 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Ten eccentric contractions of WT EDL muscle resulted in a 15.53% drop in ex vivo peak force. In contrast, this protocol reduced peak force by 20.16% in bmx EDL muscles (P = 0.0239 bmx vs. WT) and 20.86% in mdx EDL (Figure 2D)."
The genotype-defined model has greater contraction-induced force loss than wild type; it does not establish a uniform degree of protection relative to dystrophin-null muscle.
Slowly Progressive Proximal Myopathy
Proximal weakness and mobility loss progress at variable rates. In the retrospective Italian cohort, 111 of 822 patients with available ambulation information had lost walking ability at assessment. The Kaplan-Meier median age at loss of ambulation was 69 years, whereas the observed mean age among those who had already lost ambulation was 35.3 years. These are different statistics; neither describes an inevitable course for an individual. Respiratory decline can become clinically important, especially after loss of ambulation, and warrants surveillance.
Downstream
Progressive Proximal Muscle Weakness Proximal weakness is the principal clinical manifestation of the skeletal myopathy.
Waddling gait Pelvic-girdle involvement alters gait as weakness progresses.
Scoliosis Axial muscle weakness can contribute to scoliosis.
Reduced Forced Vital Capacity Extension of muscle weakness to respiratory muscles can reduce ventilatory capacity.
Show evidence (4 references)
url:https://oup.silverchair-cdn.com/article-minimal/7876416 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Information about LoA was known for 822 (87.2%) patients; of these, 111 (13.5%) patients had lost the ability to walk at the time of data collection, with a mean age at LoA of 35.3 (±15.0) years"
The full cohort report supplies the assessed denominator and observed event age, avoiding interpretation of a cross-sectional fraction as lifetime risk.
PMID:39499670 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"In 55% of patients, the diagnosis was prompted by the incidental finding of hyperCKaemia."
Incidental hyperCKaemia prompted many diagnoses in this Italian cohort; local testing practices affect the proportion.
PMID:33454189 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We analysed 190 pulmonary function measurements with a median interval of one year (1-17y) and measured a 1.00% decline of Forced Vital Capacity percent predicted (FVC%pred) per year (p = 0.004)."
The quantified rate of respiratory decline.
+ 1 more reference
Dystrophin-Deficient Cardiomyocyte Injury
Abnormal or reduced dystrophin affects cardiac as well as skeletal muscle. Cardiac disease can progress independently of skeletal muscle severity. The bmx mouse demonstrates reduced cardiac dystrophin and impaired ventricular function; human surveillance must therefore consider the heart even when skeletal weakness is mild.
cardiac muscle cell CL:0000746 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves cardiac muscle cell (CL:0000746). CL:0000746 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
Downstream
Left Ventricular Impairment and Dilated Cardiomyopathy Cardiac dystrophin dysfunction can lead to progressive ventricular impairment. The exon 45-47 rat model supports progression to cardiomyopathy, while the intervening cellular sequence is not fully established for human Becker disease.
Cardiac Intercalated Disc Disorganization The exon 45-47 rat model shows altered intercalated-disc architecture and protein localization. The contribution of dystrophin quantity versus its altered structure is unresolved.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39499670 SUPPORT BACKGROUND Human Clinical
"Becker muscular dystrophy (BMD) is an X-linked neuromuscular disease attributable to mutations in DMD, leading to a deficient and less functional dystrophin, mainly in skeletal and cardiac muscle."
Names cardiac muscle as one of the two tissues carrying the dystrophin deficit, which is the insult this node models.
Left Ventricular Impairment and Dilated Cardiomyopathy
Left ventricular systolic dysfunction and dilated cardiomyopathy are important complications, and heart failure is a major cause of mortality. In the Italian cohort, 187 of 621 assessed patients had LVEF below 55%, which measures systolic impairment rather than the prevalence of dilated cardiomyopathy. Certain deletions were associated with lower odds of impaired LVEF, but genotype does not determine an individual outcome.
Animal models
bmx exon 45-47 deletion mouse Mouse partially recapitulates Aged mice develop reduced ejection fraction and fractional shortening.
R-BMDdel45-47 rat Rat partially recapitulates Develops ventricular dilation, reduced ejection fraction, and cardiac fibrosis.
Downstream
Left Ventricular Impairment Reduced LVEF is the clinical readout of ventricular systolic dysfunction.
Dilated Cardiomyopathy Becker ventricular disease can progress to dilated cardiomyopathy and heart failure.
Show evidence (3 references)
url:https://oup.silverchair-cdn.com/article-minimal/7876416 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"One hundred and eighty-seven (30.1%) patients showed LVEF% of &lt;55%"
The full text defines the cardiac outcome as reduced ejection fraction and supplies its numerator; 621 patients had an LVEF measurement.
PMID:39499670 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Both del45-55 (P = 0.002) and del48 (P = 0.003) were significantly associated with decreased odds of developing a pathological left ventricular ejection fraction compared with del45-47."
Genotypic modulation of the cardiac endpoint within the in-frame class.
PMID:20301298 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Despite the milder skeletal muscle involvement, heart failure from DCM is a common cause of morbidity and the most common cause of death in BMD. Mean age of death is in the mid-40s."
The older GeneReviews abstract identifies heart failure as a major source of morbidity and mortality. Its summary age at death is not compared numerically with ambulation estimates from another cohort.
Cardiac Intercalated Disc Disorganization
In exon 45-47 Becker rats, TMEM65 and connexin-43 are mislocalized and intercalated discs have widened, irregular folds and abnormal contacts with nearby sarcomeres. These findings suggest impaired cardiomyocyte coupling, but no targeted rescue experiment establishes their mediation of ventricular dysfunction. This is model-derived evidence, not a demonstrated universal human cellular lesion.
Animal models
R-BMDdel45-47 rat Rat partially recapitulates Reproduces structural and protein-localization abnormalities at intercalated discs.
Downstream
Left Ventricular Impairment and Dilated Cardiomyopathy Altered mechanical and electrical coupling is a proposed contributor to cardiac dysfunction in the rat model. Co-occurrence and structural data support the hypothesis, while causal mediation remains untested.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:39358550 SUPPORT Model Organism
"At the histological level, TMEM65 notably displayed a larger distribution and mislocalization from the ICD in both BMD and DMD ventricles"
Immunostaining shows mislocalization in the rat model; reduced Tmem65 RNA did not correspond to reduced TMEM65 protein abundance in Becker rat hearts.
PMID:39358550 SUPPORT Model Organism
"we found that Cx43 was more internalized in both BMD and DMD, with a lower co-localization with N-cadherin (NCAD), an intercalated disc marker"
Connexin-43 localization changes accompany intercalated-disc disorganization in dystrophic rat hearts.
Loss of Sarcolemmal nNOS Localization
Dystrophin serves as a scaffold for sarcolemmal nNOS. Some Becker variants reduce or abolish membrane nNOS localization; other variants retain it. In a biopsy-linked human series, nNOS staining was absent in seven patients and present in six. Total nNOS abundance and residual localization also vary, including between relatives with the same splice variant.
Downstream
Impaired Exercise-Induced Vasomodulation Loss of membrane-associated nNOS weakens local nitric-oxide-dependent attenuation of sympathetic vasoconstriction during exercise. Acute rescue with interventions that augment NO-cGMP signaling supports this pathway, without proving an effect on long-term muscle decline.
RyR1-Calstabin Complex Destabilization Mislocalized cytosolic nNOS is associated with altered RyR1-calstabin regulation in del45-55 muscle biopsies. The human study supports a candidate nitrosative pathway but does not resolve causal mediation or establish this branch for every Becker variant.
Show evidence (1 reference)
url:https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4666991/ SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Sarcolemmal staining for nNOS was absent in seven BMD patients (including all five patients with deletion of exons 45–47 or 45–48, and both patients with deletion of exons 3–7) but present in the other six patients."
Human biopsies show genotype-associated variation in nNOS localization; membrane loss is not universal in Becker muscular dystrophy.
Impaired Exercise-Induced Vasomodulation
During exercise, affected muscle may fail to attenuate reflex sympathetic vasoconstriction, causing functional ischemia under experimental conditions. This abnormality is associated with nNOS membrane localization but is not present in every Becker patient. Acute tadalafil or nitrate studies demonstrate reversibility of the vascular response; its contribution to chronic weakness or disease progression remains unresolved. Reduced local NO-cGMP signaling is supported by the localization findings and pharmacological rescue rather than by a direct assay of intracellular cGMP in these patients.
nitric oxide-cGMP-mediated signaling GO:0038060 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves decreased nitric oxide-cGMP-mediated signaling (GO:0038060). GO:0038060 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology. ↓ DECREASED
Show evidence (3 references)
url:https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4666991/ SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Sympatholysis was greatly impaired in all seven patients lacking sarcolemmal nNOS but well preserved in four of six patients in whom nNOS was detected at the sarcolemma."
The biopsy-linked physiology associates loss of sarcolemmal nNOS with impaired exercise vasomodulation, with additional heterogeneity among those retaining nNOS.
PMID:23197572 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The key finding of our study is that tadalafil alleviated microvascular ischemia and fully restored blood flow regulation in eight of nine BMD patients who lacked sympatholysis when given placebo."
The randomized crossover trial demonstrates an acute physiological response in the subgroup with abnormal placebo physiology, rather than long-term clinical benefit.
url:https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4666991/ SUPPORT Human Clinical
"In the subsequent placebo‐controlled trial performed in six of the 11 patients from the open‐label trial (P2, P3, P7, P12, P14 and P16), we confirmed that sodium nitrate restores functional sympatholysis, whereas placebo was without effect"
The small randomized crossover substudy confirms an acute vascular response after nitrate supplementation.
RyR1-Calstabin Complex Destabilization
In a study of 12 Becker patients with exon 45-55 deletions, cytosolic nitric oxide production correlated with RyR1 hypernitrosylation and calstabin-1 dissociation. These findings implicate altered regulation of the sarcoplasmic-reticulum calcium-release complex. The association does not establish measured calcium leak or its causal contribution across Becker genotypes.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:22589245 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Cytosolic NO production correlated with both hypernitrosylation of the sarcoplasmic reticulum calcium-release-channel ryanodine receptor type-1 (RyR1) and release of calstabin-1, a central hub of Ca(2+) signaling and contraction in muscle."
The 12-patient del45-55 biopsy study reports correlated biochemical abnormalities; it does not establish a universal lesion or directly measure the contribution to clinical decline.

Histopathology

3
Myofiber Necrosis
Dystrophic muscle can show necrotic fibers with regeneration. In mild Becker disease, necrotic fibers may be scattered and other abnormalities subtle.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Optical microscopy examination can confirm the muscular dystrophy, demonstrati ng the association of necrosis, regeneration, and fibrosis."
The biopsy description identifies necrosis within the dystrophic pattern.
Muscle Fibrosis
Endomysial and interstitial fibrosis accompanies dystrophic remodeling and can be extensive in advanced disease, together with fatty infiltration.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Fatty infiltration and both endomy sial and interstitial extensive fibrosis are seen in the advanced stages of the disease."
The guideline describes the distribution and greater extent in advanced disease.
Variable Myofiber Size and Regeneration
Biopsy can show variable fiber size, splitting, hypercontracted fibers, regeneration, and internal nuclei. Findings may be subtle in mild disease and overlap other muscular dystrophies.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Optical microscopy examination can confirm the muscular dystrophy, demonstrati ng the association of necrosis, regeneration, and fibrosis. Marked variations in the fiber size, split fibers, and mul tinucleate and hyper -contracted bulky fib ers are also markers of dystrophinopathy"
The source describes regeneration, fiber-size variation, splitting, and abnormal nuclear and contraction morphology.
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"In mild clinical forms, m orphologic abnormalities may be subtle: moderate variations i n fiber size, scattered necrosis fibers, and rare nuclear internalizations."
Mild disease may have only subtle structural abnormalities.

Medical Actions

ACE Inhibition for Established Cardiomyopathy
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: ACE inhibitor NCIT:C247 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses ACE inhibitor (NCIT:C247). NCIT:C247 is a therapeutic agent from the NCI Thesaurus.
Platform: Small molecule
ACE inhibitors are first-line medication for established Becker cardiomyopathy in the 2023 French guideline. That guideline recommends specialist discussion of cardioprotective treatment when LVEF is below 50% or MRI shows myocardial fibrosis. It does not recommend prophylactic ACE inhibition for every Becker patient with normal cardiac findings; evidence from Duchenne muscular dystrophy does not establish that indication in Becker muscular dystrophy.
Mechanism Target:
INHIBITS Left Ventricular Impairment and Dilated Cardiomyopathy — Treats ventricular dysfunction and adverse remodeling associated with cardiomyopathy.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:20301298 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"ACE inhibitors are used with or without beta blockers for cardiomyopathy in both DMD and BMD phenotypes."
The indication for established cardiomyopathy in both phenotypes.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Thus, there is no indication to use ACE inhibitors for systematic treatment of BMD patients regardless of cardiac phenotype, such as in Duchenne MD."
The French guideline does not support universal ACE-inhibitor prophylaxis irrespective of the Becker cardiac phenotype.
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Patients with LVEF < 50% and/or evidence of myocardial fibrosis on MRI should be referre d to a specialized cardiologist in a tertiary center to discuss the initiation of cardioprotective"
Reduced LVEF or myocardial fibrosis prompts specialist assessment of cardioprotective treatment in this guideline.
Corticosteroid Therapy
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: corticosteroid CHEBI:50858 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses corticosteroid (CHEBI:50858). CHEBI:50858 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Platform: Small molecule
Corticosteroids are used in selected patients with Becker muscular dystrophy, but efficacy is less established than in Duchenne muscular dystrophy. Benefits, adverse effects, and treatment duration require individual assessment; Duchenne trial results do not establish Becker efficacy. A 24-week open-label weekly-prednisone pilot enrolled 19 participants with limb-girdle muscular dystrophy and only one with Becker muscular dystrophy; pooled biomarker and functional changes do not establish Becker-specific benefit.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:20301298 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Corticosteroid therapy improves muscle strength and function for individuals with DMD between ages five and 15 years; the same treatment is used in BMD, although the efficacy is less clear."
GeneReviews explicitly qualifies the uncertain Becker efficacy while describing clinical use.
PMID:35124660 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Out of the 20 participants, seven were female, nineteen had LGMD and one had BMD"
The mixed pilot cohort contained only one Becker participant, precluding a Becker-specific efficacy estimate.
Cardiac Transplantation
Action: Organ TransplantationNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Organ Transplantation (NCIT:C15289). NCIT:C15289 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15289
Platform: Surgery
Cardiac transplantation may be considered for terminal heart failure in selected patients with limited skeletal muscle disability and without severe respiratory failure. Selection requires multidisciplinary assessment. The French guideline generally does not recommend transplantation after loss of ambulation or with significant respiratory failure; ventricular assist devices can be discussed individually in expert centers.
Mechanism Target:
BYPASSES Left Ventricular Impairment and Dilated Cardiomyopathy — Replaces the failing heart while leaving the underlying DMD variant and extracardiac manifestations unchanged.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:20301298 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"cardiac transplantation is offered to persons with severe dilated cardiomyopathy and BMD with limited or no clinical evidence of skeletal muscle disease."
The indication and its selection criterion.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"In patients with terminal heart failure who have lost ambulation or have significant respiratory failure, cardi ac transplantati on is generally not indicated"
The guideline identifies motor and respiratory impairment as constraints on transplant selection.
Cardiac Surveillance
Action: Supportive CareNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Supportive Care (NCIT:C15747). NCIT:C15747 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15747
Platform: Other
Cardiac surveillance starts at diagnosis and continues regardless of skeletal muscle severity. Assessment includes ECG and echocardiography, with cardiac MRI to assess fibrosis and function when appropriate and Holter monitoring for cardiac dysfunction or conduction abnormalities. The 2026 GeneReviews chapter lists annual cardiac assessment for affected males and assessment before major surgery, with closer monitoring for fibrosis or dysfunction; its schedule partly draws on Duchenne care guidance. The 2023 French Becker guideline proposes assessment every two years with a normal echocardiogram and at least annually with LVEF below 50%. The interval should be individualized by cardiology. GeneReviews recommends ECG and cardiac imaging every three to five years for heterozygous females without cardiac manifestations, beginning in the second to third decade, and specialist-directed follow-up when abnormalities are present.
Show evidence (6 references)
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Cardiac monitoring must systematically include ECG and echocardiogram. The other examinations must be discussed with the patient on the basis of the patients’ symptoms and ECG and echocardiogram findings. The frequency of these assessments depends on the initial findings. The minimum interval..."
The Becker guideline specifies ECG and echocardiography and a two-year interval when the echocardiogram is normal.
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"For patie nts with LVEF < 50%, monitoring should be performed at least once a year."
Ventricular dysfunction calls for more frequent assessment.
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK1119/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK1119.pdf SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"DMD/BMD By cardiologist incl EKG & echocardiogram or cardiac MRI • Annually & before major surgery • More frequently in those age >10 yrs & those w/myocardial fibrosis or ventricular dysfunction"
GeneReviews Table 8 recommends annual and preoperative cardiac assessment; its footnote identifies Duchenne care guidelines as a basis for the intensified schedule.

Perioperative Assessment and Anaesthetic Precautions
Action: Supportive CareNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Supportive Care (NCIT:C15747). NCIT:C15747 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15747
Platform: Other
Preoperative planning should assess cardiac and respiratory involvement and muscle weakness. Avoid succinylcholine because of hyperkalaemia and rhabdomyolysis risk. Volatile anaesthetics also require specialist risk assessment because of anaesthesia-induced rhabdomyolysis in dystrophinopathies. The ENMC consensus distinguishes this complication from malignant hyperthermia and reports no association of dystrophinopathies with malignant hyperthermia susceptibility.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:35971866 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"AIR seems to be a specific complication of general anaesthesia in dystrophinopathies, especially at paediatric age. AIR can cause life‐threatening hyperkalaemia, and the risk of occurrence is possibly linked to the use of succinylcholine and/or volatile anaesthetics"
The muscular-dystrophy recommendations identify anaesthesia-induced rhabdomyolysis and hyperkalaemia as the relevant dystrophinopathy hazards.
PMID:35971866 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"There is no association with MH"
In Table 4, this statement follows the dystrophinopathy AIR recommendation and distinguishes it from malignant hyperthermia; it does not remove the need for anaesthetic precautions.
Investigational Givinostat
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: Givinostat NCIT:C71716 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses Givinostat (NCIT:C71716). NCIT:C71716 is a therapeutic agent from the NCI Thesaurus.
Platform: Small molecule
A 12-month placebo-controlled trial in 51 adults with Becker muscular dystrophy failed its primary total-fibrosis endpoint and showed no significant functional benefit. Secondary thigh and quadriceps MRI fat-fraction findings suggested a possible signal, but baseline imbalance and the small sample limit interpretation. This is investigational Becker evidence and does not establish an effective disease-modifying treatment.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:36793492 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The study failed to achieve the primary endpoint. However, there was a potential signal from the MRI assessments suggesting givinostat could prevent (or slow down) BMD disease progression."
The trial conclusion distinguishes the negative primary outcome from the exploratory interpretation of secondary MRI findings.
PMID:36793492 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Adverse events, most mild or moderate, were reported by 88.2% and 52.9% patients receiving givinostat and placebo."
Adverse events were more frequent with givinostat; the paper details platelet and triglyceride abnormalities.
Investigational Sevasemten
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: Sevasemten NCIT:C199222 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses Sevasemten (NCIT:C199222). NCIT:C199222 is a therapeutic agent from the NCI Thesaurus.
Platform: Small molecule
Sevasemten inhibits fast skeletal muscle myosin ATPase to reduce injurious contraction. A short phase 1 Becker cohort comprised five treated patients and two placebo recipients; muscle-injury biomarkers decreased but the study was too small and short to establish clinical efficacy. In a later uncontrolled 12-person study, biomarkers remained reduced and functional measures were stable over two years. These observations support investigation, not a proven treatment effect. The completed CANYON/GRAND CANYON registry had no posted results at the September 2026 review.
Mechanism Target:
INHIBITS Contraction-Induced Skeletal Muscle Injury — Investigational fast-myosin inhibition aims to reduce contraction-associated muscle injury. Sustained biomarker reductions in the small open-label Becker study support this proposed effect; they do not establish prevention of long-term functional decline.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:42308588 SUPPORT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"Treatment with sevasemten was associated with reductions in circulating biomarkers of muscle injury that were evident within 4 weeks and sustained for up to 24 months."
The 12-person study reports a durable biomarker association consistent with the proposed target effect, with no placebo control to establish efficacy.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:40452637 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"In Part C, 7 men with BMD were randomized to sevasemten (N = 5) or placebo (N = 2)"
Defines the very small Becker randomized cohort within a larger study dominated by healthy volunteers.
PMID:42308588 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"As this was an open-label trial with no placebo control, caution is required in interpretation. Additional limitations of this study include the small sample size and the lack of diversity in enrolled participants (100% White)."
The 24-month study cannot distinguish treatment benefit from natural-history variability or other uncontrolled influences.
PMID:42308588 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Treatment with sevasemten was associated with reductions in circulating biomarkers of muscle injury that were evident within 4 weeks and sustained for up to 24 months. Physical function, as assessed by North Star Ambulatory Assessment (NSAA), was stable over 24 months."
Records the observed biomarker and functional trajectories without treating them as randomized efficacy evidence.
Investigational Vamorolone
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: Vamorolone NCIT:C152825 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses Vamorolone (NCIT:C152825). NCIT:C152825 is a therapeutic agent from the NCI Thesaurus.
Platform: Small molecule
Vamorolone improved selected strength and histological measures and increased dystrophin protein in bmx mice. This preclinical study did not establish human efficacy or long-term cardiac benefit. The subsequently completed Becker phase II pilot has registry results posted in September 2026; its 24-week functional data are descriptive and do not establish a clinically meaningful benefit. The trial must be distinguished from Duchenne treatment evidence.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:37534133 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Dystrophin increases of 49% (p < 0.0001) were found in the gastrocnemius and 55% (p = 0.0173) in the heart with vamorolone treatment"
The mouse experiment shows an increase relative to vehicle-treated bmx animals, not normalization to healthy dystrophin levels or proof of human efficacy.
PMID:37534133 SUPPORT Model Organism
"to fully demonstrate causation, further studies are needed to expand upon these and previous studies."
The authors limit the proposed microRNA mechanism: reduced microRNAs and increased dystrophin correlate, but direct causal mediation was not demonstrated.
Physical Therapy and Adapted Exercise
Action: Physical TherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Physical Therapy (NCIT:C15302). NCIT:C15302 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15302
Platform: Behavioral / lifestyle
Individualized physical therapy uses gentle progressive aerobic activity, stretching, low-resistance exercise, balance work, and fall prevention to maintain function and joint mobility. Exercise intensity should account for muscle weakness, fatigue, pain, and cardiac or respiratory involvement; avoid excessive contraction and eccentric loading of weak muscles.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"This physical activity must be gentle and progressive and must take into account the specificities of BMD when choosing the exercises to offer"
The guideline recommends gradual activity adapted to Becker muscular dystrophy.
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Maintenance of segmenta l and spinal muscular and joint flexibility for analgesic pu rposes by passive mobilizations and stretching, prepar ation and recovery for exercise and proprioceptive stimulation: stretching, posture, etc."
Stretching and passive mobilization address flexibility and discomfort.
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"The most affected muscl es (those with a muscle testing MRC rating < 4) require more precautions, specifically by limiting intense contract ions and eccentric stresses and promoting aerobic metabolism."
Weak muscles require limits on intense contraction and eccentric loading.
Orthoses and Mobility Support
Action: Supportive CareNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Supportive Care (NCIT:C15747). NCIT:C15747 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15747
Platform: Other
Selected posture or walking orthoses can address deformity and support function. Wheelchairs or scooters become appropriate when walking-related falls and fractures outweigh functional benefit. Occupational and rehabilitation teams can adapt transfers, self-care, communication, and home, school, or workplace access.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Posture orthoses may be used in some patients. In the lower limbs, nocturnal posture orthoses can be proposed to address certain deformities (e.g., anti-flexum knee or antiequine orthosis)."
Night-time postural orthoses are options for selected deformities.
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Manual wheelchair; electric scooter and/or electric wh eelchair are necessary when the risks linked to walking (falls, fractures) become greater than the functional benefits."
The mobility decision balances walking benefit against injury risk.
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Specific devices facilitating meals, washing, toileting, leisur e, transfers, and environmental control can be implemented."
Assistive devices support daily activities and environmental access.
Orthopedic Surgery for Function-Limiting Deformity
Action: Surgical ProcedureNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Surgical Procedure (NCIT:C15329). NCIT:C15329 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15329
Platform: Surgery
Selected tendon or foot procedures may help walking and posture. Spinal fusion is reserved for severe forms. Rarely, macroglossia with ventilatory or swallowing consequences warrants surgery; operative planning requires cardiac, respiratory, and anesthetic assessment.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Some surgeries (e.g., Achilles tendon tenotomy, gastrocnemius aponeurotomy, tendon transfers, osteotomies or foot arthrodesis) are useful, and their u ses must be linked to functions such as walking and posture s tability. Vertebral arthrodesis is limited to the most severe forms close to DMD"
The guideline links surgery to functional impairment and limits spinal fusion to severe disease.
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"In rare cases, macroglossia surgery may be required because of functional consequences (ventilation, swallowing, etc.)."
Functionally significant macroglossia is a rare surgical indication.
Neuropsychological and Mental Health Care

Platform: Other
Assess cognitive, educational, behavioral, and emotional needs from diagnosis and when school or employment difficulties emerge. Use findings to tailor family, school, and workplace support. The French guideline recommends reassessment every two to three years in children, mental-health screening at neuromuscular visits, and formal annual psychological evaluation. Medication decisions should account for cardiac status, interactions, and adverse effects.

Respiratory Surveillance
Action: Supportive CareNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Supportive Care (NCIT:C15747). NCIT:C15747 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15747
Platform: Other
Regular evaluation includes symptoms of nocturnal hypoventilation, orthopnea, cough effectiveness, pulmonary function, and sleep investigations when indicated. The French Becker guideline recommends pulmonary function testing every two years if normal and annual specialist follow-up if abnormal. The 2026 GeneReviews dystrophinopathy schedule recommends annual respiratory evaluation and every six months after loss of ambulation, citing Duchenne care guidance. The treating team should individualize frequency to findings and progression.

"A respiratory functional exploration should be performed every 2 years, with no pulmonologist advice required at this stage if the findings are normal."
The French guideline gives a two-year interval when respiratory testing is normal.
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"In cases with an abnormal fVC, the frequency of monitoring should be ann ual. A subsequent pulmonologist assessment, performed a t least annually, will indicate the need for blood gas analysis or nightly examinations."
Abnormal FVC warrants annual or more frequent specialist assessment.

Swallowing and Nutritional Support
Action: Supportive CareNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Supportive Care (NCIT:C15747). NCIT:C15747 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15747
Platform: Other
Assess chewing, swallowing safety, weight trajectory, and nutritional intake. Adapt food texture and head position for impaired pharyngeal propulsion, provide feeding aids and speech-language input as needed, and consider oral nutritional supplements when intake is inadequate. Changes in mobility require reassessment of energy needs.
Show evidence (3 references)

"If the disorders are secondary to poor pharyngeal propulsion, the textures (mixed, adequately “slippery”) of the food and the po sition of the head during meals should be adapted."
Food texture and head position are adapted to swallowing mechanics.
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Oral nutritional supplements may be offered when increase in caloric intake becomes necessary."
Supplementation is considered when increased caloric intake is needed.
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"In the most severe patien ts there is a risk of secondary undernutrition. It is then necessary to identify all the aggravating factors: involvement of the upper limbs (speci al cutlery, raised meal plan …), macroglossia or involvement of chewing muscles (hygiene care o ral, speech therapy,..."
Nutritional assessment includes feeding mechanics, gastrointestinal problems, deformity, and ventilation-related factors.
Gastrostomy for Inadequate or Unsafe Oral Intake
Action: Surgical ProcedureNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Surgical Procedure (NCIT:C15329). NCIT:C15329 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15329
Platform: Surgery
Gastrostomy is reserved for the uncommon situation in which oral feeding cannot provide safe nutritional intake. Assess respiratory function and ventilatory requirements before placement and use a team experienced with neuromuscular disability.

Genetic Counseling and Family Evaluation
Action: Genetic CounselingNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Genetic Counseling (NCIT:C15240). NCIT:C15240 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15240
Platform: Other
Offer counseling about X-linked transmission, variable manifestations in heterozygous females, and residual recurrence risk from parental germline mosaicism. Evaluate at-risk male and female relatives, using the familial variant when known. Discuss reproductive options before pregnancy; prenatal testing is possible after variant identification, and preimplantation testing may require additional phasing.

Advanced Heart Failure Medication and Device Care
Action: Supportive CareNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Supportive Care (NCIT:C15747). NCIT:C15747 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15747
Platform: Other
Overt heart failure or ventricular systolic dysfunction requires specialist treatment using heart-failure guidance. The French Becker guideline includes ACE inhibitors or angiotensin-receptor antagonists, beta blockers, mineralocorticoid-receptor antagonists, and selected resynchronization or defibrillator therapy. Ventricular assist devices may be considered as a bridge to transplantation or destination therapy; candidacy depends on motor, respiratory, and overall clinical status.

Investigational Tadalafil
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: Tadalafil NCIT:C47743 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses Tadalafil (NCIT:C47743). NCIT:C47743 is a therapeutic agent from the NCI Thesaurus.
Platform: Small molecule
A randomized double-blind crossover experiment in ten ambulatory men with preserved cardiac function showed acute improvement of exercise blood-flow regulation. Eight of nine participants with impaired sympatholysis on placebo responded. The study measured forearm oxygenation and does not establish sustained improvement in strength, ambulation, or progression.
Mechanism Target:
INHIBITS Impaired Exercise-Induced Vasomodulation — Acutely improves the abnormal exercise vascular response; a downstream effect on chronic muscle disease has not been established.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:23197572 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The key finding of our study is that tadalafil alleviated microvascular ischemia and fully restored blood flow regulation in eight of nine BMD patients who lacked sympatholysis when given placebo."
The randomized crossover trial demonstrates an acute physiological response in the subgroup with abnormal placebo physiology, rather than long-term clinical benefit.
Show evidence (1 reference)

Sildenafil Trial with No Demonstrated Clinical Benefit
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: Sildenafil NCIT:C61940 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses Sildenafil (NCIT:C61940). NCIT:C61940 is a therapeutic agent from the NCI Thesaurus.
Platform: Small molecule
A randomized placebo-controlled crossover trial with two four-week treatment periods found no effect on blood flow, maximal work capacity, or cardiac function. Sixteen participants completed skeletal-muscle evaluations and thirteen completed cardiac MRI. These findings do not support sildenafil as an established Becker disease-modifying treatment.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:25042931 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Sixteen patients completed all skeletal muscle evaluations, and 13 completed the cardiac MRI investigations. Sildenafil had no effect on any of the outcome parameters."
The four-week crossover study found no improvement in the measured muscular or cardiac outcomes.

Diagnosis

3
DMD Genetic Testing with Reading-Frame Interpretation
Identify a pathogenic DMD variant using deletion/duplication analysis and sequencing, interpreted with the clinical phenotype and, when needed, dystrophin protein studies. Reading-frame prediction helps interpretation but does not by itself classify Becker versus Duchenne muscular dystrophy. The historical 92% concordance estimate applies to the analyzed deletion series. In unresolved cases, muscle RNA/cDNA analysis can identify splice abnormalities missed by standard DNA testing. Some urine-derived RNA approaches remain research investigations. Comprehensive assays capable of detecting sequence and copy-number variants are an alternative to sequential testing.
Genetic Testing NCIT:C15709 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (7 references)
PMID:2491009 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The correlation between phenotype and type of deletion mutation is in agreement with the "reading frame" theory in 92% of cases and is of diagnostic and prognostic significance."
The accuracy of the rule and the authors' own statement that it is diagnostically and prognostically useful.
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK1119/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK1119.pdf SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Identification of a hemizygous or heterozygous DMD variant of uncertain significance does not establish or rule out the diagnosis."
A VUS alone is insufficient for molecular confirmation.
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK1119/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK1119.pdf SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Analysis to detect copy number variants (CNVs), i.e., deletions or duplications of DMD"
The stepwise algorithm begins with deletion/duplication analysis.
+ 4 more references
Cardiac Surveillance Independent of Skeletal Muscle Severity
Evaluate cardiac involvement from diagnosis even when skeletal muscle weakness is mild. ECG and echocardiography establish baseline cardiac status; MRI can identify myocardial fibrosis before echocardiography becomes abnormal.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:20301298 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Despite the milder skeletal muscle involvement, heart failure from DCM is a common cause of morbidity and the most common cause of death in BMD."
The cardiac burden of Becker muscular dystrophy can be substantial despite relatively mild skeletal muscle disease.
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Cardiac MRI should also be discussed at diagnosis since it prov ides a more accurate estimation of cardiac function than echocardiography and can identify myocardial fibrosis, ma inly in the inferolateral wall, which can be present in patient s with a normal echocardiogram."
MRI can detect myocardial fibrosis despite a normal echocardiogram.
Muscle Biopsy and Dystrophin Protein Assessment
Muscle biopsy, immunohistochemistry, and dystrophin immunoblotting can clarify unresolved or atypical cases. Normal immunostaining does not exclude Becker muscular dystrophy; immunoblotting with antibodies against different protein regions can assess abundance and size. Biopsy is not routinely required when molecular and clinical findings already establish the diagnosis.
Muscle Biopsy NCIT:C51895 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Dystrophin immunochemistry usually shows decreased or no findings with some or all of the dedicated antibodies, but may also show normal findings. The decrease can be present in all the fibers or manifest in a fragmented manner."
The guideline warns that immunostaining can appear normal and explains why immunoblotting with multiple antibodies is informative.
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Diagnosis should be completed by western blotting of dystrophin"
The guideline recommends immunoblotting to complete dystrophin protein assessment.

Differential Diagnoses

4

Conditions with similar clinical presentations that must be differentiated from Becker Muscular Dystrophy:

Limb-Girdle Muscular Dystrophy Not Yet Curated MONDO:0016971
Overlapping Features Proximal weakness, raised CK, calf enlargement, and cardiopulmonary disease can overlap Becker muscular dystrophy.
Distinguishing Features
  • Many subtypes have autosomal inheritance and affect both sexes; the clinical pattern and causal gene distinguish them from DMD-related disease.
  • The French guideline highlights FKRP-related disease and sarcoglycanopathies; protein studies may show reduced alpha-dystroglycan or sarcoglycans. These findings require integrated molecular interpretation.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"The main differential diagnosi s included limb girdle muscular dystrophy (LGMD), facio- scapulo-humeral muscular dystrophy type 1 (FSHD 1), inflammatory myopathies and spinal muscular atrophy (SMA) are summarized in Table 1"
Table 1, inspected in the full PDF, compares FKRP-related disease and sarcoglycanopathies with Becker disease; the cached narrative identifies the differential group.
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK1119/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK1119.pdf SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Limb-girdle muscular dystrophy 1 (OMIM PS253600 & PS603511) AR AD • HyperCKemia • Proximal muscle weakness • Cardiopulmonary involvement • Impacts males & females equally • Highly variable onset that ranges from early childhood to late adulthood depending on subtype"
GeneReviews Table 3 documents proximal weakness, hyperCKemia, cardiopulmonary overlap, and autosomal inheritance affecting both sexes.
Facioscapulohumeral Muscular Dystrophy Type 1 Not Yet Curated MONDO:0008030
Overlapping Features Shoulder-girdle weakness may prompt consideration of Becker muscular dystrophy.
Distinguishing Features
  • Facial weakness, scapular winging, and asymmetric involvement favor FSHD1; the French guideline lists normal to moderately elevated CK and a D4Z4 repeat contraction mechanism.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"The main differential diagnosi s included limb girdle muscular dystrophy (LGMD), facio- scapulo-humeral muscular dystrophy type 1 (FSHD 1), inflammatory myopathies and spinal muscular atrophy (SMA) are summarized in Table 1"
The narrative identifies FSHD1; its distinguishing pattern and molecular mechanism are given in Table 1, inspected in the source PDF.
Proximal Spinal Muscular Atrophy Not Yet Curated MONDO:0019079
Overlapping Features Proximal limb weakness can overlap the motor presentation of Becker muscular dystrophy.
Distinguishing Features
  • Fasciculations, reduced or absent tendon reflexes, and a neurogenic pattern favor spinal muscular atrophy. The French table specifically describes the recessive SMN-associated form.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"The main differential diagnosi s included limb girdle muscular dystrophy (LGMD), facio- scapulo-humeral muscular dystrophy type 1 (FSHD 1), inflammatory myopathies and spinal muscular atrophy (SMA) are summarized in Table 1"
The narrative identifies SMA, and the full PDF Table 1 supplies its neurogenic distinguishing features.
Inflammatory Myopathies
Overlapping Features Acquired proximal weakness and raised CK can resemble muscular dystrophy.
Distinguishing Features
  • Subacute progression, inflammatory biopsy findings, and muscle edema on MRI support an inflammatory differential, although inflammation alone does not exclude Becker muscular dystrophy.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:37422773 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"The main differential diagnosi s included limb girdle muscular dystrophy (LGMD), facio- scapulo-humeral muscular dystrophy type 1 (FSHD 1), inflammatory myopathies and spinal muscular atrophy (SMA) are summarized in Table 1"
The narrative identifies inflammatory myopathies; Table 1 supplies subacute weakness, inflammation, and MRI edema. The biopsy text separately notes that inflammatory abnormalities can occur in Becker disease.

R-BMDdel45-47 rat
Genome-edited rats show reduced dystrophin, relatively mild skeletal muscle dysfunction, and progressive cardiac fibrosis and systolic dysfunction. Intercalated-disc proteins are mislocalized. Resting ventilatory function remains preserved at 12 months despite diaphragmatic histological changes.

click to show 
name: Becker Muscular Dystrophy
creation_date: '2026-08-23T22:30:00Z'
category: Mendelian
disease_term:
  preferred_term: Becker muscular dystrophy
  term:
    id: MONDO:0010311
    label: Becker muscular dystrophy
description: >-
  Becker muscular dystrophy is an X-linked dystrophinopathy caused by pathogenic DMD variants that permit
  production of partly functional dystrophin. Protein quantity, structure, and clinical severity vary.
  In-frame variants predominate, but the reading-frame rule is predictive rather than diagnostic: out-of-frame,
  nonsense, splice, and other variants can also produce a Becker phenotype. Progressive proximal weakness,
  calf hypertrophy, exercise-related symptoms, or incidental hyperCKaemia may lead to diagnosis. Cardiomyopathy
  can be severe despite relatively mild skeletal muscle involvement. Age at loss of ambulation and respiratory
  impairment vary substantially between patients and genotypes.
parents:
- Dystrophinopathy
- Muscular Dystrophy
pathophysiology:
- name: DMD Pathogenic Variant
  role: trigger
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: >-
    Pathogenic variants in DMD alter dystrophin quantity or function. In-frame deletions and duplications
    are common, but neither reading frame nor variant position alone establishes the Becker phenotype.
    The Italian cohort included 779 in-frame, 89 out-of-frame, and 35 nonsense variants among 903 genetically
    characterized patients. Particular exon deletions correlate with clinical outcomes at the group level;
    substantial variation remains within genotypes and families.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:3384440
    reference_title: An explanation for the phenotypic differences between patients bearing partial deletions of the DMD locus.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Deletions identified in three BMD patients are shown to maintain the translational ORF for amino
      acids and predict a shorter, lower molecular weight protein.
    explanation: >-
      The original statement of the reading-frame model, on the Becker side of the comparison.
  - reference: PMID:39499670
    reference_title: 'Natural history of Becker muscular dystrophy: DMD gene mutations predict clinical severity.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Ten per cent of patients carried out-of-frame mutations, 4% nonsense mutations and 86% in-frame
      deletions/duplications.
    explanation: >-
      The mutational composition of a 943-patient Becker cohort. It gives both the dominant in-frame class
      and the size of the exception to the rule.
  - reference: PMID:39499670
    reference_title: 'Natural history of Becker muscular dystrophy: DMD gene mutations predict clinical severity.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Patients carrying del45-49 compared with del45-47 were associated with an earlier loss of ambulation
      (P = 1 × 10-4), whereas patients with del45-55 (P = 0.005), del48 (P = 0.02) and del48-49 (P = 0.02)
      were correlated with a later loss of ambulation compared with del45-47.
    explanation: >-
      Within-class genotype-phenotype resolution: which exons are removed changes the trajectory even
      when the frame is preserved throughout.

- name: Reduced or Abnormal Partly Functional Dystrophin
  role: central_effector
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: >-
    Residual dystrophin distinguishes the Becker phenotype from severe dystrophin deficiency, but its
    abundance and function vary. Internally deleted dystrophin is a common outcome, not the only one.
    In bmx mice with deletion of Dmd exons 45-47, skeletal and cardiac dystrophin protein is reduced despite
    preserved transcript abundance. Sarcolemmal nNOS localization is lost and alpha-sarcoglycan localization
    is reduced in this model; these allele-specific observations should not be assumed for every Becker
    variant.

    and fibrosis. The severity of individual measurements need not lie uniformly between wild type and

- name: Slowly Progressive Proximal Myopathy
  role: outcome
  biological_scale: ORGANISM
  description: >-
    Proximal weakness and mobility loss progress at variable rates. In the retrospective Italian cohort,
    111 of 822 patients with available ambulation information had lost walking ability at assessment.
    The Kaplan-Meier median age at loss of ambulation was 69 years, whereas the observed mean age among
    those who had already lost ambulation was 35.3 years. These are different statistics; neither describes
    an inevitable course for an individual. Respiratory decline can become clinically important, especially
    after loss of ambulation, and warrants surveillance.

        reduced TMEM65 protein abundance in Becker rat hearts.
- name: Left Ventricular Impairment and Dilated Cardiomyopathy
  role: outcome
  biological_scale: ORGANISM
  conforms_to: cardiomyopathy_maladaptive_remodeling#Structural Cardiac Impairment and Heart Failure
  description: >-
    Left ventricular systolic dysfunction and dilated cardiomyopathy are important complications, and
    heart failure is a major cause of mortality. In the Italian cohort, 187 of 621 assessed patients had
    LVEF below 55%, which measures systolic impairment rather than the prevalence of dilated cardiomyopathy.
    Certain deletions were associated with lower odds of impaired LVEF, but genotype does not determine
    an individual outcome.

- name: Cardiac Intercalated Disc Disorganization
  role: effector
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  description: >-
    In exon 45-47 Becker rats, TMEM65 and connexin-43 are mislocalized and intercalated discs have widened,
    irregular folds and abnormal contacts with nearby sarcomeres. These findings suggest impaired cardiomyocyte
    coupling, but no targeted rescue experiment establishes their mediation of ventricular dysfunction.
    This is model-derived evidence, not a demonstrated universal human cellular lesion.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:39358550
    reference_title: Progressive cardiomyopathy with intercalated disc disorganization in a rat model of Becker dystrophy.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      At the histological level, TMEM65 notably displayed a larger distribution and mislocalization from
      the ICD in both BMD and DMD ventricles
    explanation: >-
      Immunostaining shows mislocalization in the rat model; reduced Tmem65 RNA did not correspond to
      reduced TMEM65 protein abundance in Becker rat hearts.
  - reference: PMID:39358550
    reference_title: Progressive cardiomyopathy with intercalated disc disorganization in a rat model of Becker dystrophy.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      we found that Cx43 was more internalized in both BMD and DMD, with a lower co-localization with
      N-cadherin (NCAD), an intercalated disc marker
    explanation: >-
      Connexin-43 localization changes accompany intercalated-disc disorganization in dystrophic rat hearts.
  downstream:
  - target: Left Ventricular Impairment and Dilated Cardiomyopathy
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_UNKNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      Altered mechanical and electrical coupling is a proposed contributor to cardiac dysfunction in the
      rat model. Co-occurrence and structural data support the hypothesis, while causal mediation remains
      untested.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:39358550
      reference_title: Progressive cardiomyopathy with intercalated disc disorganization in a rat model of Becker dystrophy.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: >-
        We propose that disruption in the localization of TMEM65 and Cx43 could play a role in altering
        electrical synchronization during myocardial depolarization or repolarization, due to partly inefficient
        intercellular communications.
      explanation: >-
        The authors explicitly frame this mechanism as a proposal; the edge retains that uncertainty.
- name: Loss of Sarcolemmal nNOS Localization
  role: effector
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: >-
    Dystrophin serves as a scaffold for sarcolemmal nNOS. Some Becker variants reduce or abolish membrane
    nNOS localization; other variants retain it. In a biopsy-linked human series, nNOS staining was absent
    in seven patients and present in six. Total nNOS abundance and residual localization also vary, including
    between relatives with the same splice variant.
  evidence:
  - reference: url:https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4666991/
    reference_title: Sodium nitrate alleviates functional muscle ischaemia in patients with Becker muscular dystrophy - PMC
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Sarcolemmal staining for nNOS was absent in seven BMD patients (including all five patients with
      deletion of exons 45–47 or 45–48, and both patients with deletion of exons 3–7) but present in the
      other six patients.
    explanation: >-
      Human biopsies show genotype-associated variation in nNOS localization; membrane loss is not universal
      in Becker muscular dystrophy.
  downstream:
  - target: Impaired Exercise-Induced Vasomodulation
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_KNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      Loss of membrane-associated nNOS weakens local nitric-oxide-dependent attenuation of sympathetic
      vasoconstriction during exercise. Acute rescue with interventions that augment NO-cGMP signaling
      supports this pathway, without proving an effect on long-term muscle decline.
    evidence:
    - reference: url:https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4666991/
      reference_title: Sodium nitrate alleviates functional muscle ischaemia in patients with Becker muscular dystrophy - PMC
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: >-
        Sympatholysis was greatly impaired in all seven patients lacking sarcolemmal nNOS but well preserved
        in four of six patients in whom nNOS was detected at the sarcolemma.
      explanation: >-
        The biopsy-linked physiology associates loss of sarcolemmal nNOS with impaired exercise vasomodulation,
        with additional heterogeneity among those retaining nNOS.
    - reference: PMID:23197572
      reference_title: Tadalafil alleviates muscle ischemia in patients with Becker muscular dystrophy.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: >-
        The key finding of our study is that tadalafil alleviated microvascular ischemia and fully restored
        blood flow regulation in eight of nine BMD patients who lacked sympatholysis when given placebo.
      explanation: >-
        The randomized crossover trial demonstrates an acute physiological response in the subgroup with
        abnormal placebo physiology, rather than long-term clinical benefit.
    - reference: url:https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4666991/
      reference_title: Sodium nitrate alleviates functional muscle ischaemia in patients with Becker muscular dystrophy - PMC
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: >-
        In the subsequent placebo‐controlled trial performed in six of the 11 patients from the open‐label
        trial (P2, P3, P7, P12, P14 and P16), we confirmed that sodium nitrate restores functional sympatholysis,
        whereas placebo was without effect
      explanation: >-
        The small randomized crossover substudy confirms an acute vascular response after nitrate supplementation.
  - target: RyR1-Calstabin Complex Destabilization
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_UNKNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      Mislocalized cytosolic nNOS is associated with altered RyR1-calstabin regulation in del45-55 muscle
      biopsies. The human study supports a candidate nitrosative pathway but does not resolve causal mediation
      or establish this branch for every Becker variant.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:22589245
      reference_title: Variable phenotype of del45-55 Becker patients correlated with nNOSμ mislocalization and RYR1 hypernitrosylation.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: >-
        Cytosolic NO production correlated with both hypernitrosylation of the sarcoplasmic reticulum
        calcium-release-channel ryanodine receptor type-1 (RyR1) and release of calstabin-1, a central
        hub of Ca(2+) signaling and contraction in muscle.
      explanation: >-
        The 12-patient del45-55 biopsy study reports correlated biochemical abnormalities; it does not
        establish a universal lesion or directly measure the contribution to clinical decline.
- name: Impaired Exercise-Induced Vasomodulation
  role: effector
  biological_scale: TISSUE
  description: >-
    During exercise, affected muscle may fail to attenuate reflex sympathetic vasoconstriction, causing
    functional ischemia under experimental conditions. This abnormality is associated with nNOS membrane
    localization but is not present in every Becker patient. Acute tadalafil or nitrate studies demonstrate
    reversibility of the vascular response; its contribution to chronic weakness or disease progression
    remains unresolved. Reduced local NO-cGMP signaling is supported by the localization findings and
    pharmacological rescue rather than by a direct assay of intracellular cGMP in these patients.
  biological_processes:

- name: RyR1-Calstabin Complex Destabilization
  role: effector
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: >-
    In a study of 12 Becker patients with exon 45-55 deletions, cytosolic nitric oxide production correlated
    with RyR1 hypernitrosylation and calstabin-1 dissociation. These findings implicate altered regulation
    of the sarcoplasmic-reticulum calcium-release complex. The association does not establish measured
    calcium leak or its causal contribution across Becker genotypes.
  evidence:

genetic:
- name: DMD
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: DMD
    term:
      id: hgnc:2928
      label: DMD
  relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
  inheritance:
  - name: X-linked recessive
  notes: >-
    DMD pathogenic variants cause a spectrum of dystrophinopathies. Becker classification integrates the
    clinical course and residual dystrophin with the molecular result. In-frame changes predominate, but
    out-of-frame and nonsense variants are established exceptions; reading frame alone is insufficient
    for diagnosis or individual prognosis.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:2491009
    reference_title: 'The molecular basis for Duchenne versus Becker muscular dystrophy: correlation of severity with type of deletion.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      About 60% of both Duchenne muscular dystrophy (DMD) and Becker muscular dystrophy (BMD) is due to
      deletions of the dystrophin gene.
    explanation: >-
      The historical deletion study establishes the shared DMD locus; it does not establish an absolute
      reading-frame discriminator.

inheritance:
- name: X-linked recessive
  description: >-
    Inheritance is X-linked. A heterozygous mother has a 50% chance of transmitting the variant in each
    pregnancy; an affected father transmits it to all daughters and no sons. Heterozygous females may
    have cardiac disease, muscle symptoms, or rarely a more severe dystrophinopathy phenotype. A negative
    family history does not exclude the disorder.
  inheritance_term:
    preferred_term: X-linked recessive inheritance
    term:
      id: HP:0001419
      label: X-linked recessive inheritance
  evidence:
  - reference: url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK1119/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK1119.pdf
    reference_title: https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK1119/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK1119.pdf
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      If the mother of the proband has a DMD pathogenic variant, the chance of the mother transmitting
      it in each pregnancy is 50%. If the father of the proband has a DMD pathogenic variant, he will
      transmit it to all his daughters and none of his sons.
    explanation: >-
      GeneReviews states parental transmission probabilities explicitly.
  - reference: url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK1119/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK1119.pdf
    reference_title: https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK1119/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK1119.pdf
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      Heterozygous females are typically asymptomatic or may exhibit weakness, fatigue, and cardiac involvement.
    explanation: >-
      The female phenotype extends beyond isolated cardiac risk and is variably expressed.
